		<div id="mw-indicator-coordinates" class="mw-indicator"><div class="mw-parser-output"><span id="coordinates"><a href="/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system" title="Geographic coordinate system">Coordinates</a>: <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1156832818">.mw-parser-output .geo-default,.mw-parser-output .geo-dms,.mw-parser-output .geo-dec{display:inline}.mw-parser-output .geo-nondefault,.mw-parser-output .geo-multi-punct,.mw-parser-output .geo-inline-hidden{display:none}.mw-parser-output .longitude,.mw-parser-output .latitude{white-space:nowrap}</style><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Clipperton_Island&amp;params=10_18_N_109_13_W_region:FR-CP_type:isle"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">10°18′N</span> <span class="longitude">109°13′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">10.300°N 109.217°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">10.300; -109.217</span></span></span></a></span></span></div></div>

					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><p class="mw-empty-elt">
</p>
<div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Atoll of Overseas France</div>
<p class="mw-empty-elt">

</p>

431,273&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (166,515&#160;sq&#160;mi)</li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Clipperton Island</b> (French: <i lang="fr">La Passion–Clipperton</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French">[la pasjɔ̃ klipœʁtɔn]</a></span>; Spanish: <i lang="es">Isla de la Pasión</i>), also known as <b>Clipperton Atoll</b><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> and previously referred to as <b>Clipperton's Rock</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-Morrell1841_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Morrell1841-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> is an 8.9&#160;<a href="/wiki/Square_kilometre" title="Square kilometre">km<sup>2</sup></a> (3.4&#160;<a href="/wiki/Square_mile" title="Square mile">sq&#160;mi</a>) uninhabited <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">French</a> coral <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atoll</a> in the eastern <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a>. The only French territory in the North Pacific, Clipperton is 10,675&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (6,633&#160;<a href="/wiki/Mile" title="Mile">mi</a>) from <a href="/wiki/Paris" title="Paris">Paris</a>, <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>; 5,400&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (2,900&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) from <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>; and 1,280&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (690&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) from <a href="/wiki/Acapulco" title="Acapulco">Acapulco</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mexico" title="Mexico">Mexico</a>.
</p><p>Clipperton was documented by French merchant-explorers in 1711 and formally claimed as part of the French protectorate of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tahiti" title="Kingdom of Tahiti">Tahiti</a> in 1858. Despite this, American guano miners began working the island in the early 1890s. As interest in the island grew, Mexico asserted a claim to the island based upon Spanish records from the 1520s that may have identified the island. Mexico established a small military colony on the island in 1905, but during the <a href="/wiki/Mexican_Revolution" title="Mexican Revolution">Mexican Revolution</a> contact with the mainland became infrequent, most of the colonists died, and lighthouse keeper Victoriano Álvarez instituted a short, brutal reign as "king" of the island. Eleven survivors were rescued in 1917 and Clipperton was abandoned.
</p><p>The dispute between Mexico and France over Clipperton was taken to binding <a href="/wiki/International_arbitration" title="International arbitration">international arbitration</a> in 1909 with the Italian king, <a href="/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_III_of_Italy" title="Victor Emmanuel III of Italy">Victor Emmanuel III</a>, deciding in 1931 that the island was French territory. Despite the ruling, Clipperton remained largely uninhabited until 1944 when the U.S. Navy established a weather station on the island to support its war efforts in the Pacific. France protested and as concerns about Japanese activity in the eastern Pacific waned the U.S. abandoned the site in late 1945.
</p><p>Since the end of World War II, Clipperton has primarily been the site for scientific expeditions to study the island's wildlife and marine life, including its significant <a href="/wiki/Masked_booby" title="Masked booby">masked</a> and <a href="/wiki/Brown_booby" title="Brown booby">brown booby</a> colonies. It has also hosted climate scientists and amateur radio <a href="/wiki/DX-pedition" title="DX-pedition">DX-peditions</a>. Plans to develop the island for trade and tourism have been considered, but none have been enacted and the island remains mostly uninhabited with periodic visits from the French navy.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png/220px-Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png/330px-Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png/440px-Localisation_de_l%27ile_de_Clipperton.png 2x" data-file-width="783" data-file-height="551" /></a><figcaption>Location of Clipperton Island</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Coral_island" title="Coral island">coral island</a> is located at <span class="geo-inline"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1156832818"><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Clipperton_Island&amp;params=10_18_N_109_13_W_type:isle_region:FR-CP&amp;title=Clipperton+Island"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">10°18′N</span> <span class="longitude">109°13′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="vcard"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">10.300°N 109.217°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">10.300; -109.217</span></span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; (<span class="fn org">Clipperton Island</span>)</span></span></span></a></span></span> in the East Pacific, 1,080&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (583&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) southwest of Mexico, 2,424&#160;km (1,309&#160;nmi) west of <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a>, 2,545&#160;km (1,374&#160;nmi) west of <a href="/wiki/Costa_Rica" title="Costa Rica">Costa Rica</a> and 2,390&#160;km (1,290&#160;nmi) northwest of the <a href="/wiki/Gal%C3%A1pagos_Islands" title="Galápagos Islands">Galápagos Islands</a> in <a href="/wiki/Ecuador" title="Ecuador">Ecuador</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The nearest land is <a href="/wiki/Socorro_Island" title="Socorro Island">Socorro Island</a>, about 945&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (510&#160;<a href="/wiki/Nautical_mile" title="Nautical mile">nmi</a>) to the south-east in the <a href="/wiki/Revillagigedo_Islands" title="Revillagigedo Islands">Revillagigedo Archipelago</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The nearest French-owned island is <a href="/wiki/Hiva_Oa" title="Hiva Oa">Hiva Oa</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Marquesas_Islands" title="Marquesas Islands">Marquesas Islands</a> of <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia" title="French Polynesia">French Polynesia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite its proximity to North America, Clipperton is often considered one of the eastern-most points of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a> due to being part the French Indo-Pacific,<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> and to commonalities between its marine fauna and the marine fauna of <a href="/wiki/Hawaii" title="Hawaii">Hawaii</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kiribati" title="Kiribati">Kiribati</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Line_Islands" title="Line Islands">Line Islands</a>, with the island sitting along the migration path for animals in the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Tropical_Pacific" class="mw-redirect" title="Eastern Tropical Pacific">Eastern Tropical Pacific</a> region.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Romero-Torres_Treml_Acosta_Paz-García_2018_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Romero-Torres_Treml_Acosta_Paz-García_2018-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-clip_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-clip-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> The island is the only emerged part of the <a href="/wiki/East_Pacific_Rise" title="East Pacific Rise">East Pacific Rise</a>, as well as the only feature in the <a href="/wiki/Clipperton_Fracture_Zone" title="Clipperton Fracture Zone">Clipperton Fracture Zone</a> that breaks the ocean's surface, and it is one of the few islands in the Pacific that lacks an underwater <a href="/wiki/Archipelagic_apron" title="Archipelagic apron">archipelagic apron</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The atoll is low-lying and largely barren, with some scattered grasses, and a few clumps of <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconut palms</a> (<i>Cocos nucifera</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-Sacotte1978_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sacotte1978-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> The land ring surrounding the lagoon measures 1.7 square kilometres (0.66&#160;sq&#160;mi) in area with an average elevation of 2&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (6.6&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>), although a small <a href="/wiki/Volcano" title="Volcano">volcanic</a> outcropping, referred to as <a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Rock&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Clipperton Rock (page does not exist)">Clipperton Rock</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocher_de_Clipperton" class="extiw" title="fr:Rocher de Clipperton">fr</a>&#93;</span> (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Rocher de Clipperton</i></span>), rises to 29&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (95&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) on its southeast side.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The surrounding 3.7-square-kilometre (1.4&#160;sq&#160;mi) reef hosts an abundance of corals and is partly exposed at low tide.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Clipperton_Rock_(1899).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Clipperton_Rock_%281899%29.jpg/220px-Clipperton_Rock_%281899%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="105" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Clipperton_Rock_%281899%29.jpg/330px-Clipperton_Rock_%281899%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Clipperton_Rock_%281899%29.jpg/440px-Clipperton_Rock_%281899%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1620" data-file-height="776" /></a><figcaption>1899 sketch of Clipperton Rock from the <i>Bulletin of the Museum of Comparative Zoology at Harvard College</i>, after a photograph</figcaption></figure>
<p>Clipperton Rock is the remains of the island's now extinct volcano's rim;<sup id="cite_ref-:1_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> because it includes this rocky outcropping, Clipperton is not a true atoll and is sometimes referred to as a 'near-atoll'.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The surrounding reef in combination with the weather makes landing on the island difficult and anchoring offshore hazardous for larger ships; in the 1940s American ships reported active problems in this regard.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Fluckey2012_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fluckey2012-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The environment of Clipperton Island has been studied extensively with the first recordings and sample collection being done in the 1800s.<sup id="cite_ref-:6_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Modern research on Clipperton is focused primarily on climate science and migratory wildlife.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The SURPACLIP oceanographic expedition, a joint undertaking by the <a href="/wiki/National_Autonomous_University_of_Mexico" title="National Autonomous University of Mexico">National Autonomous University of Mexico</a> and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_New_Caledonia" title="University of New Caledonia">University of New Caledonia Nouméa</a>, made extensive studies of the island in 1997.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> In 2001, <a href="/wiki/French_National_Centre_for_Scientific_Research" title="French National Centre for Scientific Research">French National Centre for Scientific Research</a> geographer <a href="/wiki/Christian_Jost_(geographer)" title="Christian Jost (geographer)">Christian Jost</a> extended the 1997 studies through the French Passion 2001 expedition, which focused on the evolution of Clipperton's <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem" title="Ecosystem">ecosystem</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Bienvenue_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bienvenue-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> In 2003, cinematrographer Lance Milbrand stayed on the island for 41&#160;days, recording the adventure for the <i><a href="/wiki/National_Geographic_Explorer" title="National Geographic Explorer">National Geographic Explorer</a></i> and plotting a <a href="/wiki/GPS" class="mw-redirect" title="GPS">GPS</a> map of Clipperton for the <a href="/wiki/National_Geographic_Society" title="National Geographic Society">National Geographic Society</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2005, a four-month scientific mission organised by <a href="/wiki/Jean-Louis_%C3%89tienne" title="Jean-Louis Étienne">Jean-Louis Étienne</a> made a complete inventory of Clipperton's mineral, plant, and animal species; studied algae as deep as 100&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (328&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) below sea level; and examined the effects of pollution.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> A 2008 expedition from the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Washington" title="University of Washington">University of Washington</a>'s School of Oceanography collected sediment cores from the lagoon to study climate change over the past millennium.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Lagoon">Lagoon</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Lagoon">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:2022-04-20-00_00_2022-04-20-23_59_Sentinel-2_L2A_True_color.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/2022-04-20-00_00_2022-04-20-23_59_Sentinel-2_L2A_True_color.jpg/220px-2022-04-20-00_00_2022-04-20-23_59_Sentinel-2_L2A_True_color.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/2022-04-20-00_00_2022-04-20-23_59_Sentinel-2_L2A_True_color.jpg/330px-2022-04-20-00_00_2022-04-20-23_59_Sentinel-2_L2A_True_color.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/2022-04-20-00_00_2022-04-20-23_59_Sentinel-2_L2A_True_color.jpg/440px-2022-04-20-00_00_2022-04-20-23_59_Sentinel-2_L2A_True_color.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1008" data-file-height="635" /></a><figcaption>Clipperton Island photographed by the <a href="/wiki/Sentinel-2" title="Sentinel-2">Sentinel-2</a> satellite.</figcaption></figure><p>Clipperton is a ring-shaped <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atoll</a> that completely encloses a <a href="/wiki/Water_stagnation" title="Water stagnation">stagnant</a> <a href="/wiki/Fresh_water" title="Fresh water">fresh water</a> lagoon and measures 12&#160;<a href="/wiki/Kilometre" title="Kilometre">km</a> (7.5&#160;<a href="/wiki/Mile" title="Mile">mi</a>) in circumference and 720 hectares (2.8&#160;sq&#160;mi) in area.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJost2003_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJost2003-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The island is the only coral island in the eastern Pacific.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> The lagoon is devoid of fish, and is shallow over parts of the eroded coral heads,<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> but contains some deep basins with depths of 43–72&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (141–236&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>), including a spot known as <span title="French-language text"><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Trou Sans Fond</span></span> ('the bottomless hole') with <a href="/wiki/Acid" title="Acid">acidic</a> water at its base. The water is described as being almost fresh at the surface and highly <a href="/wiki/Eutrophication" title="Eutrophication">eutrophic</a>. Seaweed beds cover approximately 45&#160;per&#160;cent of the lagoon's surface. The rim averages 150&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (490&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) in width, reaching 400&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (1,300&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) in the west, and narrowing to 45&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (148&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) in the north-east, where sea waves occasionally spill over into the lagoon.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Ten <a href="/wiki/Islets" class="mw-redirect" title="Islets">islets</a> are present in the lagoon, six of which are covered with vegetation, including the Egg Islands (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">les îles aux Œufs</i></span>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJostFriedlanderBallesterosBrown201612,_18_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJostFriedlanderBallesterosBrown201612,_18-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The closure of the lagoon approximately 170 years ago and prevention of seawater from entering the lagoon has formed a <a href="/wiki/Meromictic_lake" title="Meromictic lake">meromictic lake</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:10_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> The surface of the lagoon has a high concentration of phytoplankton that vary slightly with the seasons.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> As a result of this the water columns are stratified and do not mix leaving the lagoon with an <a href="/wiki/Oxic" class="mw-redirect" title="Oxic">oxic</a> and brackish upper water layer and a deep sulfuric anoxic saline layer.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_49-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFluckey201217_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFluckey201217-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Fluckey2004_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fluckey2004-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> At a depth of approximately 15&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (49&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) the water shifts with <a href="/wiki/Salinity" title="Salinity">salinity</a> rising and both <a href="/wiki/PH" title="PH">pH</a> and oxygen quickly decreasing.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_49-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> The deepest levels of the lagoon record waters enriched with hydrogen sulfide which prevent the growth of coral. Before the lagoon was closed off to seawater, coral and clams were able to survive in the area as evident by fossilized specimens.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 112">&#58;&#8202;112&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>Studies of the water have found that microbial communities on the water's surface are similar to other water samples from around the world with deeper water samples showing a great diversity of both bacteria and <a href="/wiki/Archaea" title="Archaea">archaea</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_49-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> In 2005, a group of French scientists discovered three <a href="/wiki/Dinoflagellate" title="Dinoflagellate">dinoflagellate</a> microalgae species in the lagoon: <i>Peridiniopsis cristata</i>, which was abundant; <i><a href="/wiki/Durinskia" title="Durinskia">Durinskia baltica</a></i>, which was known to exist previously in other locations, but was new to Clipperton; and <i>Peridiniopsis cristata</i> var. <i>tubulifera</i>, which is unique to the island.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The lagoon also harbours millions of <a href="/wiki/Isopod" class="mw-redirect" title="Isopod">isopods</a>, which are reported to deliver a painful sting.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While some sources have rated the lagoon water as non-<a href="/wiki/Drinking_water" title="Drinking water">potable</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> testimony from the crew of the tuna clipper M/V <i>Monarch,</i> stranded for 23&#160;days in 1962 after their boat sank, indicates otherwise. Their report reveals that the lagoon water, while "muddy and dirty", was drinkable, despite not tasting very good. Several of the castaways drank it, with no apparent ill effects.<sup id="cite_ref-No94_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-No94-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Survivors of a Mexican military colony in 1917 (see below) indicated that they were dependent upon rain for their water supply, catching it in old boats.<sup id="cite_ref-No94_58-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-No94-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> American servicemen on the island during World War II had to use evaporators to purify the lagoon's water.<sup id="cite_ref-Fluckey2012_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fluckey2012-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Aside from the lagoon and water caught from rain, no freshwater sources are known to exist.<sup id="cite_ref-:10_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:10-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_49-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The island has a tropical oceanic climate, with average temperatures of 20–32&#160;<a href="/wiki/Celsius" title="Celsius">°C</a> (68–90&#160;<a href="/wiki/Fahrenheit" title="Fahrenheit">°F</a>) and highs up to 37.8&#160;<a href="/wiki/Celsius" title="Celsius">°C</a> (100.0&#160;<a href="/wiki/Fahrenheit" title="Fahrenheit">°F</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-:6_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Annual rainfall is 3,000 to 5,000 millimetres (120 to 200&#160;in), and the humidity level is generally between 85&#160;per&#160;cent and 95&#160;per&#160;cent with December to March being the drier months. The prevailing winds are the southeast <a href="/wiki/Trade_winds" title="Trade winds">trade winds</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Bienvenue_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bienvenue-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Tchékémian2021_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tchékémian2021-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The rainy season occurs from May to October,<sup id="cite_ref-:6_31-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:6-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> and the region is subject to tropical cyclones from April to September, but such storms often pass to the northeast of Clipperton.<sup id="cite_ref-Bienvenue_36-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bienvenue-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> In 1997 Clipperton was in the path of the start of <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Felicia_(1997)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane Felicia (1997)">Hurricane Felicia</a>, as well as <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Sandra_(2015)" title="Hurricane Sandra (2015)">Hurricane Sandra</a> in 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> In addition, Clipperton has been subjected to multiple tropical storms and depressions including <a href="/wiki/Tropical_Storm_Andres_(2003)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical Storm Andres (2003)">Tropical Storm Andres</a> in 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>  Surrounding ocean waters are warm, pushed by equatorial and counter-equatorial currents and have seen temperature increases due to global warming.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora_and_fauna">Flora and fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Flora and fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg/220px-Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg/330px-Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg/440px-Jielbeaumadier_crabe_de_clipperton_mjp_paris_2014.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a><figcaption>A bright-orange <a href="/wiki/Johngarthia_oceanica" title="Johngarthia oceanica">Clipperton crab</a> (<i>Johngarthia oceanica</i>)</figcaption></figure>
<p>When Snodgrass and Heller visited in 1898, they reported that "no land plant is native to the island".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESnodgrassHeller1902_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESnodgrassHeller1902-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Historical accounts from 1711, 1825, and 1839 show a low grassy or suffrutescent (partially woody) flora. During <a href="/wiki/Marie-H%C3%A9l%C3%A8ne_Sachet" title="Marie-Hélène Sachet">Marie-Hélène Sachet</a><span class="nowrap">&#39;s</span> visit in 1958, the vegetation was found to consist of a sparse cover of spiny grass and low thickets, a creeping plant (<i><a href="/wiki/Ipomoea" title="Ipomoea">Ipomoea</a> spp.</i>), and stands of coconut palm. This low-lying herbaceous flora seems to be a pioneer in nature, and most of it is believed to be composed of recently introduced species. Sachet suspected that <i><a href="/wiki/Heliotropium_curassavicum" title="Heliotropium curassavicum">Heliotropium curassavicum</a></i>, and possibly <i><a href="/wiki/Portulaca_oleracea" title="Portulaca oleracea">Portulaca oleracea</a></i>, were native. <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">Coconut</a> palms and <a href="/wiki/Pig" title="Pig">pigs</a> introduced in the 1890s by <a href="/wiki/Guano" title="Guano">guano</a> miners were still present in the 1940s.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFluckey201217_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFluckey201217-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The largest coconut grove is Bougainville Wood (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Bois de Bougainville</i></span>) on the southwestern end of the island.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> On the northwest side of the atoll, the most abundant plant species are <i><a href="/wiki/Cenchrus_echinatus" title="Cenchrus echinatus">Cenchrus echinatus</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Sida_rhombifolia" title="Sida rhombifolia">Sida rhombifolia</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Corchorus" title="Corchorus">Corchorus</a> aestuans</i>. These plants compose a shrub cover up to 30&#160;<a href="/wiki/Centimetre" title="Centimetre">cm</a> (12&#160;<a href="/wiki/Inch" title="Inch">in</a>) in height, and are intermixed with <i><a href="/wiki/Eclipta_(plant)" title="Eclipta (plant)">Eclipta</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Phyllanthus" title="Phyllanthus">Phyllanthus</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Solanum" title="Solanum">Solanum</a></i>, as well as the taller <i><a href="/wiki/Brassica_juncea" title="Brassica juncea">Brassica juncea</a></i>. The islets in the lagoon are primarily vegetated with <a href="/wiki/Cyperaceae" title="Cyperaceae">Cyperaceae</a>, <a href="/wiki/Scrophulariaceae" title="Scrophulariaceae">Scrophulariaceae</a>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Ipomoea_pes-caprae" title="Ipomoea pes-caprae">Ipomoea pes-caprae</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJostFriedlanderBallesterosBrown201612_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJostFriedlanderBallesterosBrown201612-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> A unique feature of Clipperton is that the vegetation is arranged in parallel rows of species, with dense rows of taller species alternating with lower, more open vegetation. This was assumed to be a result of the trench-digging method of <a href="/wiki/Phosphate" title="Phosphate">phosphate</a> mining used by guano hunters.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_25-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The only land animals known to exist are two species of reptiles (the <a href="/wiki/Gehyra_insulensis" class="mw-redirect" title="Gehyra insulensis">Pacific stump-toed gecko</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Emoia_cyanura" title="Emoia cyanura">copper-tailed skink</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> bright-orange land crabs known as <a href="/wiki/Johngarthia_oceanica" title="Johngarthia oceanica">Clipperton crabs</a> (<i>Johngarthia oceanica</i>, prior to 2019 classified as <i>Johngartia planata</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> birds, and <a href="/wiki/Black_rats" class="mw-redirect" title="Black rats">ship rats</a>. The rats probably arrived when large fishing boats wrecked on the island in 1999 and 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitman_et_al._2005_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitman_et_al._2005-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The pigs introduced in the 1890s reduced the crab population, which in turn allowed <a href="/wiki/Grassland" title="Grassland">grassland</a> to gradually cover about 80&#160;per&#160;cent of the land surface.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> The elimination of these pigs in 1958, the result of a personal project by <a href="/wiki/Kenneth_E._Stager" title="Kenneth E. Stager">Kenneth E. Stager</a>, caused most of this vegetation to disappear as the population of land crabs recovered.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitman_et_al._2005_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitman_et_al._2005-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> As a result, Clipperton is virtually a sandy desert with only 674&#160;palms counted by <a href="/wiki/Christian_Jost_(geographer)" title="Christian Jost (geographer)">Christian Jost</a> during the Passion 2001 French mission and five islets in the lagoon with grass that the terrestrial crabs cannot reach. A 2005 report by the <a href="/wiki/National_Oceanic_and_Atmospheric_Administration" title="National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration">National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration</a><span class="nowrap">&#39;s</span> Southwest Fisheries Science Center indicated that the increased rat presence had led to a decline in both crab and bird populations, causing a corresponding increase in both vegetation and coconut palms. This report urgently recommended eradication of rats, so that vegetation might be reduced, and the island might return to its 'pre-human' state.<sup id="cite_ref-Pitman_et_al._2005_73-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pitman_et_al._2005-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1825, <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Morrell" title="Benjamin Morrell">Benjamin Morrell</a> reported finding green sea turtles nesting on Clipperton, but later expeditions have not found nesting turtles there, possibly due to disruption from guano extraction, as well as the introduction of pigs and rats. Sea turtles found on the island appear to have been injured due to fishing practices.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Morrell also reported fur and elephant seals on the island in 1825, but they too have not been recorded by later expeditions.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELorvelecPascalFretey2009_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELorvelecPascalFretey2009-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Morrell1841_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Morrell1841-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Enchelynassa_canina_head.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Enchelynassa_canina_head.jpg/220px-Enchelynassa_canina_head.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Enchelynassa_canina_head.jpg/330px-Enchelynassa_canina_head.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Enchelynassa_canina_head.jpg/440px-Enchelynassa_canina_head.jpg 2x" data-file-width="633" data-file-height="421" /></a><figcaption>The head of a <a href="/wiki/Enchelynassa_canina" class="mw-redirect" title="Enchelynassa canina">viper moray</a> (<i>Enchelynassa canina</i>)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Birds are common on the island; Morrell noted in 1825: "The whole island is literally covered with sea-birds, such as gulls, whale-birds, gannets, and the booby".<sup id="cite_ref-Morrell1841_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Morrell1841-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Thirteen species of birds are known to breed on the island and 26 others have been observed as visitors.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The island has been identified as an <a href="/wiki/Important_Bird_Area" title="Important Bird Area">Important Bird Area</a> by <a href="/wiki/BirdLife_International" title="BirdLife International">BirdLife International</a> because of the large breeding colony of masked boobies, with 110,000&#160;individual birds recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>  Observed bird species include <a href="/wiki/White_tern" title="White tern">white terns</a>, <a href="/wiki/Masked_booby" title="Masked booby">masked boobies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sooty_tern" title="Sooty tern">sooty terns</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brown_booby" title="Brown booby">brown boobies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brown_noddy" title="Brown noddy">brown noddies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Black_noddy" title="Black noddy">black noddies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Greater_frigatebird" class="mw-redirect" title="Greater frigatebird">great frigatebirds</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coot" title="Coot">coots</a>, <a href="/wiki/Swallow" title="Swallow">martins</a> (swallows), <a href="/wiki/Cuckoo" title="Cuckoo">cuckoos</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Yellow_warbler" title="Yellow warbler">yellow warblers</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Ducks and moorhens have been reported in the lagoon.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonIsland_25-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonIsland-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The coral reef on the north side of the island includes colonies more than 2 metres (6.6&#160;ft) high. The 2018 Tara Pacific expedition located five colonies of <i><a href="/wiki/Millepora_platyphylla" title="Millepora platyphylla">Millepora platyphylla</a></i> at depths of 28–32 metres (92–105&#160;ft), the first of this fire coral species known in the region. Among the <i><a href="/wiki/Porites" title="Porites">Porites</a> spp.</i> stony corals, some <a href="/wiki/Coral_bleaching" title="Coral bleaching">bleaching</a> was observed, along with other indications of disease or stress, including parasitic worms and microalgae.<sup id="cite_ref-:5_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The reefs that surround Clipperton have some of the highest concentration of <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic species</a> found anywhere with more than 115 species identified.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllenRobertson1997_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllenRobertson1997-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> Many species are recorded in the area, including five or six endemics, such as <a href="/wiki/Clipperton_angelfish" title="Clipperton angelfish">Clipperton angelfish</a> (<i>Holacanthus limbaughi</i>), Clipperton grouper (<i><a href="/wiki/Epinephelus" title="Epinephelus">Epinephelus</a> clippertonensis</i>), Clipperton damselfish (<i><a href="/wiki/Stegastes" title="Stegastes">Stegastes</a> baldwini</i>) and Robertson's wrasse (<i><a href="/wiki/Thalassoma_robertsoni" title="Thalassoma robertsoni">Thalassoma robertsoni</a></i>). Widespread species around the reefs include <a href="/wiki/Paranthias_colonus" title="Paranthias colonus">Pacific creolefish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Blue_and_gold_snapper" title="Blue and gold snapper">blue-and-gold snapper</a>, and various species of <a href="/wiki/Mulloidichthys" title="Mulloidichthys">goatfish</a>. In the water column, <a href="/wiki/Caranx" title="Caranx">trevallies</a> are predominant, including <a href="/wiki/Caranx_lugubris" title="Caranx lugubris">black jacks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bigeye_trevally" title="Bigeye trevally">bigeye trevally</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bluefin_trevally" title="Bluefin trevally">bluefin trevally</a>. Also common around Clipperton are <a href="/wiki/Black_triggerfish" title="Black triggerfish">black triggerfish</a>;, several species of <a href="/wiki/Grouper" title="Grouper">groupers</a>, including <a href="/wiki/Dermatolepis_dermatolepis" title="Dermatolepis dermatolepis">leather bass</a> and <a href="/wiki/Epinephelus_labriformis" title="Epinephelus labriformis">starry groupers</a>; <a href="/wiki/Mexican_hogfish" title="Mexican hogfish">Mexican hogfish</a>; <a href="/wiki/Whitecheek_surgeonfish" title="Whitecheek surgeonfish">whitecheek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Acanthurus_triostegus" title="Acanthurus triostegus">convict</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ctenochaetus_marginatus" title="Ctenochaetus marginatus">striped-fin surgeonfish</a>; <a href="/wiki/Yellow_longnose_butterflyfish" title="Yellow longnose butterflyfish">yellow longnose</a> and <a href="/wiki/Johnrandallia" title="Johnrandallia">blacknosed butterflyfish</a>; <a href="/wiki/Coral_hawkfish" title="Coral hawkfish">coral hawkfish</a>; <a href="/wiki/Arothron_meleagris" title="Arothron meleagris">golden pufferfish</a>; <a href="/wiki/Moorish_idol" title="Moorish idol">Moorish idols</a>; <a href="/wiki/Parrotfish" title="Parrotfish">parrotfish</a>; and moray eels, especially <a href="/wiki/Speckled_moray_eel" title="Speckled moray eel">speckled moray eels</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECluaAuratBinBin201854–57_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECluaAuratBinBin201854–57-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> The population of sharks in the waters around the island was noted to have increased in both density and size of individuals in a 2019 expedition, particularly the population of the <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_whitetip_shark" title="Oceanic whitetip shark">white tip shark</a>. <a href="/wiki/Galapagos_shark" title="Galapagos shark">Galapagos sharks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Triaenodon_obesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Triaenodon obesus">reef sharks</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hammerhead_shark" title="Hammerhead shark">hammerhead sharks</a> are also present around Clipperton.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECluaAuratBinBin201862–63_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECluaAuratBinBin201862–63-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Three expeditions to Clipperton have collected <a href="/wiki/Sponge" title="Sponge">sponge</a> specimens, including U.S. President <a href="/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt" title="Franklin D. Roosevelt">Franklin Roosevelt</a>'s visit in 1938. Of the 190 specimens collected, 20 species were noted, including nine found only at Clipperton. One of the endemic sponges, collected during the 1938 visit, was named <i>Callyspongia roosevelti</i> in honor of Roosevelt.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
In April 2009, Steven Robinson, a tropical fish dealer from <a href="/wiki/Hayward,_California" title="Hayward, California">Hayward, California</a>, traveled to Clipperton to collect <a href="/wiki/Clipperton_angelfish" title="Clipperton angelfish">Clipperton angelfish</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Upon his return to the United States, he described the 52 illegally collected fish to federal wildlife authorities as <a href="/wiki/King_angelfish" class="mw-redirect" title="King angelfish">king angelfish</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-:7_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:7-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:8_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> not the rarer Clipperton angelfish, which he intended to sell for $10,000.<sup id="cite_ref-:8_93-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:8-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:9_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:9-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> On 15 December 2011, Robinson was sentenced to 45 days of incarceration, one year of probation, and a $2,000 fine.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup></p><figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sichem_Osprey.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Sichem_Osprey.jpg/220px-Sichem_Osprey.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="194" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Sichem_Osprey.jpg/330px-Sichem_Osprey.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/Sichem_Osprey.jpg/440px-Sichem_Osprey.jpg 2x" data-file-width="899" data-file-height="791" /></a><figcaption>Freighter <i>Sichem Osprey</i> grounded on Clipperton Island in 2010.</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environmental_threats">Environmental threats</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Environmental threats">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>During the night of 10&#160;February 2010, the <i>Sichem Osprey</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> a Maltese <a href="/wiki/Chemical_tanker" title="Chemical tanker">chemical tanker</a>, ran aground en route from the <a href="/wiki/Panama_Canal" title="Panama Canal">Panama Canal</a> to <a href="/wiki/South_Korea" title="South Korea">South Korea</a>. The 170&#160;<a href="/wiki/Metre" title="Metre">m</a> (558&#160;<a href="/wiki/Foot_(unit)" title="Foot (unit)">ft</a>) ship contained 10,513 metric tons (11,589 short tons) of <a href="/wiki/Xylene" title="Xylene">xylene</a>, 6,005 metric tons (6,619 short tons) of soybean oil, and 6,000 metric tons (6,600 short tons) of tallow.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 43">&#58;&#8202;43&#8202;</span></sup> All 19&#160;crew members were reported safe, and the vessel reported no leaks.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> The vessel was re-floated on 6&#160;March<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> and returned to service.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In mid-March 2012, the crew from the Clipperton Project noted the widespread presence of refuse, particularly on the northeast shore, and around the Clipperton Rock. Debris, including plastic bottles and containers, create a potentially harmful environment for the island's flora and fauna. This trash is common to only two beaches (northeast and southwest), and the rest of the island is fairly clean. Other refuse has been left after the occupations by Americans 1944–1945, French 1966–1969, and the 2008 scientific expedition.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> During a 2015 scientific and amateur radio expedition to Clipperton, the operating team discovered a package that contained 1.2 kilograms (2.6&#160;lb) of cocaine. It is suspected that the package washed up after being discarded at sea.<sup id="cite_ref-Tchékémian2021_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tchékémian2021-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> In April 2023, the Passion 23 mission by France's <a href="/w/index.php?title=Armed_Forces_in_the_Antilles&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Armed Forces in the Antilles (page does not exist)">Armed Forces in the Antilles</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forces_arm%C3%A9es_aux_Antilles" class="extiw" title="fr:Forces armées aux Antilles">fr</a>&#93;</span> and the surveillance frigate <i><a href="/wiki/French_frigate_Germinal" title="French frigate Germinal">Germinal</a></i> collected more than 200 kilograms (440&#160;lb) of plastic waste from the island's beaches along with a bale of cocaine.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Sea_Around_Us_(organization)" title="Sea Around Us (organization)">Sea Around Us Project</a> estimates the Clipperton EEZ produces a harvest of 50,000 metric tons (55,000 short tons) of fish per year; however, because French naval patrols in the area are infrequent, this includes a significant amount of illegal fishing, along with lobster harvesting and <a href="/wiki/Shark_finning" title="Shark finning">shark finning</a>, resulting in estimated losses for France of €0.42 per kilogram of fish caught.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As deep-sea mining of <a href="/wiki/Polymetallic_nodule" class="mw-redirect" title="Polymetallic nodule">polymetallic nodules</a> increases in the adjacent <a href="/wiki/Clipperton_Fracture_Zone" title="Clipperton Fracture Zone">Clarion–Clipperton Zone</a>, similar mining activity within France's <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> surrounding the atoll may have an impact on marine life around Clipperton. Polymetallic nodules were discovered in the Clipperton EEZ during the Passion 2015 expedition.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Politics and government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island is an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_France" title="Overseas France">overseas state private property of France</a> under direct authority of the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_the_Overseas_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of the Overseas (France)">Minister of the Overseas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Although the island is French territory, it has no status within the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Ownership of Clipperton Island was disputed in the 19th and early 20th centuries between <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mexico" title="Mexico">Mexico</a>, but was finally settled through <a href="/wiki/Arbitration" title="Arbitration">arbitration</a> in 1931; the 'Clipperton Island Case' remains widely studied in <a href="/wiki/International_law" title="International law">international law</a> textbooks.<sup id="cite_ref-Dickinson1933_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dickinson1933-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the late 1930s, as <a href="/wiki/Flying_boat" title="Flying boat">flying boats</a> opened the Pacific to air travel, Clipperton Island was noted as a possible waypoint for a trans-Pacific route from the Americas to Asia via the <a href="/wiki/Marquesas_Islands" title="Marquesas Islands">Marquesas Islands</a> in French Polynesia, bypassing Hawaii. However, France indicated no interest in developing commercial air traffic in the corridor.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After France ratified the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Convention_on_the_Law_of_the_Sea" title="United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea">United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea</a> (UNCLOS) in 1996,<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> they reaffirmed the <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone_of_France" title="Exclusive economic zone of France">exclusive economic zone</a> off Clipperton island which had been established in 1976.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> After changes were made to the area nations were allowed to claim under the third convention of UNCLOS<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> France in 2018 expanded the outer limits of the territorial sea to 22&#160;km (12&#160;nmi) and the exclusive economic zone off Clipperton Island to 370&#160;km (200&#160;nmi), encompassing 431,273 square kilometres (166,515&#160;sq&#160;mi) of ocean.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>On 21 February 2007, administration of Clipperton was transferred from the <a href="/wiki/High_commissioner" title="High commissioner">High Commissioner</a> of the Republic in <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia" title="French Polynesia">French Polynesia</a> to the Minister of Overseas France.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 99">&#58;&#8202;99&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>In 2015, French MP <a href="/wiki/Philippe_Folliot" title="Philippe Folliot">Philippe Folliot</a> set foot on Clipperton becoming the first elected official from France to do so. Folliot noted that visiting Clipperton was something he had wanted to do since he was nine years old.<sup id="cite_ref-:4_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-119">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2022, France passed legislation officially referring to the island as "La Passion–Clipperton".<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Plan_de_l%27Isle_de_la_Passion_(1711).png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Plan_de_l%27Isle_de_la_Passion_%281711%29.png/220px-Plan_de_l%27Isle_de_la_Passion_%281711%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="175" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Plan_de_l%27Isle_de_la_Passion_%281711%29.png/330px-Plan_de_l%27Isle_de_la_Passion_%281711%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Plan_de_l%27Isle_de_la_Passion_%281711%29.png/440px-Plan_de_l%27Isle_de_la_Passion_%281711%29.png 2x" data-file-width="577" data-file-height="458" /></a><figcaption>Sketch of "l'Isle de la Passion" (Clipperton) from <i>La Princesse</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em;">&#39;s</span> ship's diary (1711).</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Discovery_and_early_claims">Discovery and early claims</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Discovery and early claims">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>There are several claims to the first discovery of the island. The earliest recorded possible sighting is 24 January 1521 when Portuguese-born Spanish explorer <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_Magellan" title="Ferdinand Magellan">Ferdinand Magellan</a> discovered an island he named San Pablo after turning westward away from the American mainland during his circumnavigation of the globe.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> On 15&#160;November 1528, <a href="/wiki/Spaniards" title="Spaniards">Spaniard</a> <a href="/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_de_Saavedra_Cer%C3%B3n" title="Álvaro de Saavedra Cerón">Álvaro de Saavedra Cerón</a> discovered an island he called Isla Médanos in the region while on an expedition commissioned by his cousin, the Spanish conquistador <a href="/wiki/Hern%C3%A1n_Cort%C3%A9s" title="Hernán Cortés">Hernán Cortés</a>, to find a route to the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Vargas,_2011_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vargas,_2011-125">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although both San Pablo and Isla Médanos are considered to be possible sightings of Clipperton, the island was first charted by French merchant <a href="/wiki/Michel_Dubocage" title="Michel Dubocage">Michel Dubocage</a>, commanding <i>La Découverte</i>, who arrived at the island on <a href="/wiki/Good_Friday" title="Good Friday">Good Friday</a>, 3&#160;April 1711; he was joined the following day by fellow ship captain <a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_de_Chassiron&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Martin de Chassiron (page does not exist)">Martin de Chassiron</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathieu_Martin_de_Chassiron" class="extiw" title="fr:Mathieu Martin de Chassiron">fr</a>&#93;</span> and <i>La Princesse</i>. The island was given the name <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Île de la Passion</i></span> ('Passion Island') as the date of rediscovery fell within <a href="/wiki/Passiontide" title="Passiontide">Passiontide</a>. They drew up the first map of the island and claimed it for France.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In August 1825, American sea captain <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Morrell" title="Benjamin Morrell">Benjamin Morrell</a> made the first recorded landing on Clipperton, exploring the island and making a detailed report of its vegetation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESachet1962b286_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESachet1962b286-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The common name for the island comes from <a href="/wiki/John_Clipperton" title="John Clipperton">John Clipperton</a>, an English <a href="/wiki/Pirate" class="mw-redirect" title="Pirate">pirate</a> and <a href="/wiki/Privateer" title="Privateer">privateer</a> who fought the Spanish during the early 18th century, and who is said to have passed by the island. Some sources claim that he used it as a base for his raids on shipping.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="19th_century">19th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: 19th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span id="Mexican_claim_1821.E2.80.931858"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Mexican_claim_1821–1858">Mexican claim 1821–1858</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Mexican claim 1821–1858">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>After its <a href="/wiki/Declaration_of_Independence_(Mexico)" title="Declaration of Independence (Mexico)">declaration of independence</a> in 1821, Mexico took possession of the lands that had once belonged to Spain. As Spanish records noted the existence of the island as early as 1528, the territory was incorporated into Mexico.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Mexico" title="Constitution of Mexico">Mexican constitution of 1917</a> explicitly includes the island, using the Spanish name <span title="Spanish-language text"><i lang="es">La Pasión</i></span>, as Mexican territory. This would be amended on January 18, 1934, after the sovereignty dispute over the island was settled in favor of France.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:$1_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/%241_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg/220px-%241_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="241" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/02/%241_stamp_of_Clipperton_Island.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="325" data-file-height="356" /></a><figcaption>1895 $1 stamp of Clipperton Island, issued by W. Frese &amp; Co. as an agent of the Oceanic Phosphate Company. The <a href="/wiki/Local_post" title="Local post">local post stamps</a> were used for mail travelling between Clipperton and San Francisco.<sup id="cite_ref-CIstamps_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIstamps-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<div style="padding:1em 3em 1em 3em"><div style="display:flex;flex-flow:row wrap; gap:3em"><div style="flex: 1 0 300px;"><b>Original text</b> <b>:</b><br /> El territorio nacional comprende el de las partes integrantes de la Federación y además el de las islas adyacentes en ambos mares. Comprende, asimismo, la isla de Guadalupe, las de Revillagigedo y la de la Pasión, situadas en el océano Pacífico.
</div><div style="flex: 1 0 300px;"><b>Translation:</b><br /> The national territory includes that of the integral parts of the Federation and also that of the adjacent islands in both seas. It also includes the island of Guadalupe, Revillagigedo and La Pasión, located in the Pacific Ocean.
</div></div><p style="clear:left;margin:1em 0 1em 6em;align-self:flex-end">&#8212; Mexican Constitution of 1917 <sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p></div>
<h4><span id="French_claim_.281858.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="French_claim_(1858)">French claim (1858)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: French claim (1858)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>On 17&#160;November 1858, <a href="/wiki/Emperor_of_the_French" title="Emperor of the French">Emperor</a> <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_III" title="Napoleon III">Napoleon III</a> annexed Clipperton as part of the French protectorate of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tahiti" title="Kingdom of Tahiti">Tahiti</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Ship-of-the-line Lieutenant Victor Le Coat de Kervéguen published a notice of this annexation in Hawaiian newspapers to further cement France's claim to the island.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Guano_mining_claims_.281892.E2.80.931905.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Guano_mining_claims_(1892–1905)">Guano mining claims (1892–1905)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Guano mining claims (1892–1905)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 1892, a claim on the island was filed with the U.S. State Department under the U.S. <a href="/wiki/Guano_Islands_Act" title="Guano Islands Act">Guano Islands Act</a> by Frederick W. Permien of San Francisco on behalf of the Stonington Phosphate Company.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> In 1893, Permien transferred those rights to a new company, the Oceanic Phosphate Company.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> In response to the application, the State Department rejected the claim, noting France's prior claim on the island and that the claim was not bonded as was required by law.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Additionally during this time there were concerns in Mexico that the British or Americans would lay claim to the island.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite the lack of U.S. approval of its claim, the Oceanic Phosphate Company began mining guano on the island in 1895.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESecretaría_de_Relaciones_Exteriores_de_México19093–4_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESecretaría_de_Relaciones_Exteriores_de_México19093–4-141">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> Although the company had plans for as many as 200 workers on the island, at its peak only 25 men were stationed there.<sup id="cite_ref-CIstamps_132-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIstamps-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The company shipped its guano to Honolulu and San Francisco where it sold for between US$10 and US$20 per ton.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT18980311_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT18980311-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> In 1897, the Oceanic Phosphate Company began negotiations with the British <a href="/wiki/John_T._Arundel#Pacific_Islands_Company_Ltd" title="John T. Arundel">Pacific Islands Company</a> to transfer its interest in Clipperton; this drew the attention of both French and Mexican officials.<sup id="cite_ref-SGQ1980_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SGQ1980-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 24&#160;November 1897, French naval authorities arrived on the <i><a href="/wiki/French_cruiser_Duguay-Trouin_(1877)" title="French cruiser Duguay-Trouin (1877)">Duguay Trouin</a></i> and found three Americans working on the island. The French ordered the <a href="/wiki/American_flag" class="mw-redirect" title="American flag">American flag</a> to be lowered.<sup id="cite_ref-SGQ1980_143-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SGQ1980-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> At that time, U.S. authorities assured the French that they did not intend to assert American sovereignty over the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Dickinson1933_109-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dickinson1933-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-AJIL26_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AJIL26-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> A few weeks later, on 13&#160;December 1897, Mexico sent the gunboat <i>La Demócrata</i> and a group of marines to assert its claim on the island, evicting the Americans, raising the Mexican flag, and drawing a protest from France.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERogers1933278–279_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERogers1933278–279-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> From 1898 to 1905, the Pacific Islands Company worked the Clipperton guano deposits under a concession agreement with Mexico.<sup id="cite_ref-SGQ1980_143-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SGQ1980-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> In 1898, Mexico made a US$1.5 million claim against the Oceanic Phosphate Company for the guano shipped from the island from 1895 to 1897.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT18980311_142-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT18980311-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span id="Mexican_colonization_.281905.E2.80.931917.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Mexican_colonization_(1905–1917)">Mexican colonization (1905–1917)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Mexican colonization (1905–1917)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 1905, the Mexican government renegotiated its agreement with the British Pacific Islands Company, establishing a military garrison on the island a year later and erecting a <a href="/wiki/Lighthouse" title="Lighthouse">lighthouse</a> under the orders of <a href="/wiki/List_of_Presidents_of_Mexico" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Presidents of Mexico">Mexican President</a> <a href="/wiki/Porfirio_D%C3%ADaz" title="Porfirio Díaz">Porfirio Díaz</a>. Captain <a href="/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Arnaud" title="Ramón Arnaud">Ramón Arnaud</a> was appointed governor of Clipperton. At first he was reluctant to accept the post, believing it amounted to exile from Mexico, but he relented after being told that Díaz had personally chosen him to protect Mexico's interests in the international conflict with France. It was also noted that because Arnaud spoke English, French, and Spanish, he would be well equipped to help protect Mexico's sovereignty over the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-SGQ1980_143-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SGQ1980-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> He arrived on Clipperton as governor later that year.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By 1914 around 100 men, women, and children lived on the island, resupplied every two months by a ship from <a href="/wiki/Acapulco" title="Acapulco">Acapulco</a>. With the escalation of fighting in the <a href="/wiki/Mexican_Revolution" title="Mexican Revolution">Mexican Revolution</a>, regular resupply visits ceased, and the inhabitants were left to their own devices.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonProject_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonProject-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> On 28 February 1914, the schooner <i>Nokomis</i> wrecked on Clipperton; with a still seaworthy lifeboat, four members of the crew volunteered to row to Acapulco for help.<sup id="cite_ref-Perrill1937_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Perrill1937-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/USS_Cleveland_(C-19)" title="USS Cleveland (C-19)">USS&#160;<i>Cleveland</i></a> arrived months later to rescue the crew.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> While there, the captain offered to transport the survivors of the colony back to Acapulco; Arnaud refused as he believed a supply ship would soon arrive.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg/220px-SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="131" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg/330px-SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg/440px-SobrevivientesClipperton.jpg 2x" data-file-width="786" data-file-height="467" /></a><figcaption>Mexican survivors from Clipperton Island, 1917</figcaption></figure>
<p>By 1917, all but one of the male inhabitants had died. Many had perished from <a href="/wiki/Scurvy" title="Scurvy">scurvy</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> while others, including Arnaud, died during an attempt to sail after a passing ship to fetch help.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> Lighthouse keeper Victoriano Álvarez was the last man on the island, together with 15 women and children.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> Álvarez proclaimed himself 'king', and began a campaign of rape and murder, before being killed by Tirza Rendón, who was his favourite victim. Almost immediately after Álvarez's death, four women and seven children, the last survivors, were picked up by the U.S. Navy gunship <a href="/wiki/USS_Yorktown_(PG-1)" title="USS Yorktown (PG-1)"><i>Yorktown</i></a> on 18 July 1917.<sup id="cite_ref-ClippertonProject_149-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ClippertonProject-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Final_arbitration_of_ownership_.281931.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Final_arbitration_of_ownership_(1931)">Final arbitration of ownership (1931)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Final arbitration of ownership (1931)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Throughout Mexico's occupation of Clipperton, France insisted on its ownership of the island, and lengthy diplomatic correspondence between the two countries led to a treaty on 2&#160;March 1909, agreeing to seek binding <a href="/wiki/International_arbitration" title="International arbitration">international arbitration</a> by <a href="/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_III_of_Italy" title="Victor Emmanuel III of Italy">Victor Emmanuel III of Italy</a>, with each nation promising to abide by his determination.<sup id="cite_ref-Dickinson1933_109-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dickinson1933-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> In 1931, Victor Emmanuel III issued his arbitral decision in the 'Clipperton Island Case', declaring Clipperton a French possession.<sup id="cite_ref-AJIL26_144-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AJIL26-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Mexican President <a href="/wiki/Pascual_Ortiz_Rubio" title="Pascual Ortiz Rubio">Pascual Ortiz Rubio</a>, in response to public opinion that considered the Italian king biased towards France, consulted international experts on the validity of the decision, but ultimately Mexico accepted Victor Emmanuel's findings.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> France formally took possession of Clipperton on January 26, 1935.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="U.S._presidential_visit">U.S. presidential visit</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Clipperton_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: U.S. presidential visit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
